```html
<thinking-panel _ngcontent-ng-c91144647="" _nghost-ng-c542629960="" class="ng-tns-c542629960-29 ng-tns-c91144647-27 ng-trigger ng-trigger-expandCollapse ng-star-inserted" aria-expanded="false" aria-hidden="true" style="height: 0px; opacity: 0;"><div _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" class="thinking-panel ng-tns-c542629960-29 ng-trigger ng-trigger-thinkingPanelAnimation"><div _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" class="item-container ng-tns-c542629960-29 ng-star-inserted" style=""><div _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" class="item-side ng-tns-c542629960-29"><div _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" class="icon-container ng-tns-c542629960-29 ng-star-inserted"><mat-icon _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" role="img" fonticon="spark" class="mat-icon notranslate ng-tns-c542629960-29 google-symbols mat-ligature-font mat-icon-no-color" aria-hidden="true" data-mat-icon-type="font" data-mat-icon-name="spark"></mat-icon></div><!----><mat-divider _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" role="separator" class="mat-divider item-side-line ng-tns-c542629960-29 is-first mat-divider-vertical" aria-orientation="vertical"></mat-divider></div><thought-item _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" class="ng-tns-c542629960-29" _nghost-ng-c672901147=""><div _ngcontent-ng-c672901147="" data-test-id="thought-header" class="thought-header gds-body-m gds-italic">Policy Reversion and Notice Limitations</div><div _ngcontent-ng-c672901147="" data-test-id="thought-body" class="gds-body-m gds-italic">I am currently evaluating the strategic impact of the Civil Rights Office’s decision to maintain a narrow scope for the investigation. I’ve observed a significant procedural shift where the investigator clarified that the case is governed by a 2016 grievance procedure rather than the initially proposed 2026 policy. This limits the opportunity to review evidence before findings are made, making the initial definitions of the nature of the charges even more critical. I am synthesizing the investigator’s promise to consider all theories against the potential risk that excluding key administrators from the formal notice might procedurally block findings against them later.</div><!----></thought-item></div><div _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" class="item-container ng-tns-c542629960-29 ng-star-inserted" style=""><div _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" class="item-side ng-tns-c542629960-29"><!----><mat-divider _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" role="separator" class="mat-divider item-side-line ng-tns-c542629960-29 mat-divider-vertical" aria-orientation="vertical"></mat-divider></div><thought-item _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" class="ng-tns-c542629960-29" _nghost-ng-c672901147=""><div _ngcontent-ng-c672901147="" data-test-id="thought-header" class="thought-header gds-body-m gds-italic">The Characterization Conflict</div><div _ngcontent-ng-c672901147="" data-test-id="thought-body" class="gds-body-m gds-italic">I am investigating the tension between the University’s characterization of certain administrative roles as customer service and their legal obligations during the interactive accommodation process. The evidence suggests a fundamental conflict involving administrators' categorical claims that support for ADHD-related executive function is not an accommodation but a personal responsibility. I am working to resolve how this characterization aligns with or violates established civil rights standards, particularly given that the University has previously awarded scholarships specifically for the coaching they now claim is outside their purview.</div><!----></thought-item></div><div _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" class="item-container ng-tns-c542629960-29 ng-star-inserted" style=""><div _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" class="item-side ng-tns-c542629960-29"><!----><mat-divider _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" role="separator" class="mat-divider item-side-line ng-tns-c542629960-29 mat-divider-vertical" aria-orientation="vertical"></mat-divider></div><thought-item _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" class="ng-tns-c542629960-29" _nghost-ng-c672901147=""><div _ngcontent-ng-c672901147="" data-test-id="thought-header" class="thought-header gds-body-m gds-italic">Future Strategic Investigation</div><div _ngcontent-ng-c672901147="" data-test-id="thought-body" class="gds-body-m gds-italic">My next steps involve a rigorous search for administrative precedents and legal guidance regarding the adequacy of investigation standards. I am thinking through how to formalize a rebuttal that preserves the right to appeal the scope of the investigation if the final report fails to address the omitted actors. I will be generating and testing three competing hypotheses to determine if the current investigative approach is a standard administrative practice or a tactical narrowing designed to protect the institution from complex findings of discrimination.</div><!----></thought-item></div><!----><!----><!----><!----><!----><!----><!----><!----><div _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" class="item-container Scholarship@Western</div></a></browse-web-chip><!----><!----><!----><!----></div></div><!----></browse-chip-list></div><!----><!----><!----><div _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" class="item-container ng-tns-c542629960-29 ng-star-inserted" style=""><div _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" class="item-side ng-tns-c542629960-29"><div _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" class="icon-container ng-tns-c542629960-29 ng-star-inserted"><mat-icon _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" role="img" fonticon="spark" class="mat-icon notranslate ng-tns-c542629960-29 google-symbols mat-ligature-font mat-icon-no-color" aria-hidden="true" data-mat-icon-type="font" data-mat-icon-name="spark"></mat-icon></div><!----><mat-divider _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" role="separator" class="mat-divider item-side-line ng-tns-c542629960-29 is-first mat-divider-vertical" aria-orientation="vertical"></mat-divider></div><thought-item _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" class="ng-tns-c542629960-29" _nghost-ng-c672901147=""><div _ngcontent-ng-c672901147="" data-test-id="thought-header" class="thought-header gds-body-m gds-italic">Procedural Boundaries and Timelines</div><div _ngcontent-ng-c672901147="" data-test-id="thought-body" class="gds-body-m gds-italic">I have pinpointed the specific constraints of the 2016 grievance procedure, which dictates a strict sixty-day window for investigation and a brief ten-day period for contesting findings. Understanding these rigid timelines is vital, as it highlights a potential strategic risk: if the formal notice remains narrow, the evidence must be flawlessly integrated into the record now to avoid being procedurally barred from the final determination.</div><!----></thought-item></div><div _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" class="item-container ng-tns-c542629960-29 ng-star-inserted" style=""><div _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" class="item-side ng-tns-c542629960-29"><!----><mat-divider _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" role="separator" class="mat-divider item-side-line ng-tns-c542629960-29 mat-divider-vertical" aria-orientation="vertical"></mat-divider></div><thought-item _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" class="ng-tns-c542629960-29" _nghost-ng-c672901147=""><div _ngcontent-ng-c672901147="" data-test-id="thought-header" class="thought-header gds-body-m gds-italic">Administrative Accountability and Record Preservation</div><div _ngcontent-ng-c672901147="" data-test-id="thought-body" class="gds-body-m gds-italic">I am scrutinizing the investigator's claim that the final report will eventually reflect theories and witnesses currently excluded from the formal charges. This creates a tension between the 'general notice' purpose of the investigation and the actual depth of the fact-finding process. I am determining if this approach is a standard administrative deferral or a tactical move to limit the institutional footprint of the investigation by omitting specific high-level staff members from the formal allegations.</div><!----></thought-item></div><div _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" class="item-container ng-tns-c542629960-29 ng-star-inserted" style=""><div _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" class="item-side ng-tns-c542629960-29"><!----><mat-divider _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" role="separator" class="mat-divider item-side-line ng-tns-c542629960-29 mat-divider-vertical" aria-orientation="vertical"></mat-divider></div><thought-item _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" class="ng-tns-c542629960-29" _nghost-ng-c672901147=""><div _ngcontent-ng-c672901147="" data-test-id="thought-header" class="thought-header gds-body-m gds-italic">Direct Analysis of Investigative Strategy</div><div _ngcontent-ng-c672901147="" data-test-id="thought-body" class="gds-body-m gds-italic">I will now proceed to synthesize the detailed objections and internal strategy documents to identify exactly which administrative interactions are at risk of being minimized. By cross-referencing these documents with the core policy requirements, I intend to build a framework that ensures every evidentiary proffer is captured in the official record, preparing the groundwork for any necessary procedural appeals or external oversight.</div><!----></thought-item></div><!----><!----><!----><!----><!----><!----><!----><div _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" class="item-container ng-tns-c542629960-29 ng-star-inserted" style=""><div _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" class="item-side ng-tns-c542629960-29"><div _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" class="icon-container ng-tns-c542629960-29 ng-star-inserted"><mat-icon _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" role="img" fonticon="spark" class="mat-icon notranslate ng-tns-c542629960-29 google-symbols mat-ligature-font item-side-line ng-tns-c542629960-29 is-first mat-divider-vertical" aria-orientation="vertical"></mat-divider></div><thought-item _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" class="ng-tns-c542629960-29" _nghost-ng-c672901147=""><div _ngcontent-ng-c672901147="" data-test-id="thought-header" class="thought-header gds-body-m gds-italic">Premature Determinations and Policy Mandates</div><div _ngcontent-ng-c672901147="" data-test-id="thought-body" class="gds-body-m gds-italic">I am synthesizing the risk that labeling administrative interactions as 'customer service' functions as a premature legal determination rather than a neutral investigative step. By analyzing the 2016 procedure, I have found that while the initial notice is intended to be general, applying such definitive labels before the fact-finding process is complete may unfairly bias the eventual findings of harm and findings of fact that the policy explicitly mandates. I have observed that this characterization effectively bypasses the interactive accommodation process by pre-sorting direct evidence into a non-jurisdictional category.</div><!----></thought-item></div><div _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" class="item-container ng-tns-c542629960-29 ng-star-inserted" style=""><div _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" class="item-side ng-tns-c542629960-29"><!----><mat-divider _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" role="separator" class="mat-divider item-side-line ng-tns-c542629960-29 mat-divider-vertical" aria-orientation="vertical"></mat-divider></div><thought-item _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" class="ng-tns-c542629960-29" _nghost-ng-c672901147=""><div _ngcontent-ng-c672901147="" data-test-id="thought-header" class="thought-header gds-body-m gds-italic">Temporal Integrity and Continuous Violations</div><div _ngcontent-ng-c672901147="" data-test-id="thought-body" class="gds-body-m gds-italic">I am resolving a critical contradiction regarding the 180-day reporting rule and its application to what appears to be a continuous series of linked events. The current investigative stance risks 'fracturing' the narrative of the case by treating interconnected events as isolated incidents. This is a significant procedural gap, as it could result in the exclusion of core evidence regarding a long-term failure to accommodate, even though that evidence is vital to proving a pattern of discriminatory behavior.</div><!----></thought-item></div><div _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" class="item-container ng-tns-c542629960-29 ng-star-inserted" style=""><div _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" class="item-side ng-tns-c542629960-29"><!----><mat-divider _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" role="separator" class="mat-divider item-side-line ng-tns-c542629960-29 mat-divider-vertical" aria-orientation="vertical"></mat-divider></div><thought-item _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" class="ng-tns-c542629960-29" _nghost-ng-c672901147=""><div _ngcontent-ng-c672901147="" data-test-id="thought-header" class="thought-header gds-body-m gds-italic">Strategic Hypothesis Testing</div><div _ngcontent-ng-c672901147="" data-test-id="thought-body" class="gds-body-m gds-italic">I will now transition to testing three competing hypotheses to determine if the refusal to amend the formal charges is a standard procedural preference for simplicity or a tactical narrowing designed to limit institutional liability. I am thinking through how to leverage federal standards and state civil rights guidance to build a robust record that prevents the investigator from omitting key participants in the final analysis, ensuring that the 'theories of the case' are as defensible as the raw evidence.</div><!----></thought-item></div><!----><!----><!----><!----><!----><!----><div _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" ng-tns-c542629960-29 ng-star-inserted" style=""><div _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" class="item-side ng-tns-c542629960-29"><div _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" class="icon-container ng-tns-c542629960-29 aria-hidden="true" data-mat-icon-type="font" data-mat-icon-name="spark"></mat-icon></div><!----><mat-divider _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" role="separator" class="mat-divider item-side-line ng-tns-c542629960-29 is-first mat-divider-vertical" aria-orientation="vertical"></mat-divider></div><thought-item _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" class="ng-tns-c542629960-29" _nghost-ng-c672901147=""><div _ngcontent-ng-c672901147="" data-test-id="thought-header" class="thought-header gds-body-m gds-italic">Categorization as Pre-Judgment</div><div _ngcontent-ng-c672901147="" data-test-id="thought-body" class="gds-body-m gds-italic">I am thinking through the investigator’s use of the 'customer service' label for administrative interactions involving accommodation management. My analysis suggests this isn't just a neutral descriptor; it functions as a premature legal conclusion that could shield specific staff members from the accountability mandated by the 2016 grievance process. By pre-sorting core civil rights functions into a non-jurisdictional category, the university risks bypassing the rigorous scrutiny required to determine if a failure to accommodate actually occurred.</div><!----></thought-item></div><div _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" class="item-container ng-tns-c542629960-29 ng-star-inserted" style=""><div _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" class="item-side ng-tns-c542629960-29"><!----><mat-divider _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" role="separator" class="mat-divider item-side-line ng-tns-c542629960-29 mat-divider-vertical" aria-orientation="vertical"></mat-divider></div><thought-item _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" class="ng-tns-c542629960-29" _nghost-ng-c672901147=""><div _ngcontent-ng-c672901147="" data-test-id="thought-header" class="thought-header gds-body-m gds-italic">Procedural Gaps in Notice Scope</div><div _ngcontent-ng-c672901147="" data-test-id="thought-body" class="gds-body-m gds-italic">I've identified a significant tension between the investigator's claim that the current notice is merely 'general' and the promise that the final report will 'reflect' the broader theories of the case. There is a critical information gap regarding how evidence against excluded respondents will be translated into formal findings if they are not named in the initial charges. I am synthesizing a strategy to ensure that the promise to 'reflect' these elements becomes a binding procedural hook, preventing the final report from omitting the roles of high-level administrators in the alleged discriminatory pattern.</div><!----></thought-item></div><div _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" class="item-container ng-tns-c542629960-29 ng-star-inserted" style=""><div _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" class="item-side ng-tns-c542629960-29"><!----><mat-divider _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" role="separator" class="mat-divider item-side-line ng-tns-c542629960-29 mat-divider-vertical" aria-orientation="vertical"></mat-divider></div><thought-item _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" class="ng-tns-c542629960-29" _nghost-ng-c672901147=""><div _ngcontent-ng-c672901147="" data-test-id="thought-header" class="thought-header gds-body-m gds-italic">Accountability and Evidentiary Mapping</div><div _ngcontent-ng-c672901147="" data-test-id="thought-body" class="gds-body-m gds-italic">I am transitioning to a meticulous review of the intake transcripts and the log of existing submissions to identify any previous admissions regarding the scope of the case. I intend to map out specific evidentiary links for each witness, ensuring the record is so comprehensive that the investigator cannot ignore the interconnected nature of the events. My next steps involve analyzing whether the final report can legally expand beyond the initial notice to capture misconduct discovered during the fact-finding process, thereby preserving the path for full remediation.</div><!----></thought-item></div><!----><!----><!----><!----><!----><!----><div _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" class="item-container ng-tns-c542629960-29 ng-star-inserted" style=""><div _nghost-ng-c672901147=""><div _ngcontent-ng-c672901147="" data-test-id="thought-header" class="thought-header gds-body-m gds-italic">Policy Protections Against Investigator Bias</div><div _ngcontent-ng-c672901147="" data-test-id="thought-body" class="gds-body-m gds-italic">I have identified a specific provision in the university's governing anti-discrimination procedure that mandates the timely resolution of concerns regarding investigator bias or conflicts of interest. This is a critical finding, as it establishes a formal mechanism to challenge the current investigative direction if the refusal to expand the formal charges is perceived as a departure from neutral fact-finding. I am synthesizing how this mandate for impartiality interacts with the investigator's discretion to define the scope of the case.</div><!----></thought-item></div><div _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" class="item-container ng-tns-c542629960-29 ng-star-inserted" style=""><div _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" class="item-side ng-tns-c542629960-29"><!----><mat-divider _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" role="separator" class="mat-divider item-side-line ng-tns-c542629960-29 mat-divider-vertical" aria-orientation="vertical"></mat-divider></div><thought-item _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" class="ng-tns-c542629960-29" _nghost-ng-c672901147=""><div _ngcontent-ng-c672901147="" data-test-id="thought-header" class="thought-header gds-body-m gds-italic">The Investigative Scope and Hearing Eligibility</div><div _ngcontent-ng-c672901147="" data-test-id="thought-body" class="gds-body-m gds-italic">I am resolving a significant contradiction regarding the relationship between the initial notice of charges and the right to a formal hearing. My analysis of the procedural rules suggests that the final hearing is strictly tied to the findings within the investigation report; therefore, a restricted initial notice risks creating a procedural barrier that could shield omitted administrators from being held accountable in a later hearing. I am thinking through the risk that accepting a 'general' notice now might result in the permanent exclusion of core evidence from the final determination.</div><!----></thought-item></div><div _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" class="item-container ng-tns-c542629960-29 ng-star-inserted" style=""><div _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" class="item-side ng-tns-c542629960-29"><!----><mat-divider _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" role="separator" class="mat-divider item-side-line ng-tns-c542629960-29 mat-divider-vertical" aria-orientation="vertical"></mat-divider></div><thought-item _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" class="ng-tns-c542629960-29" _nghost-ng-c672901147=""><div _ngcontent-ng-c672901147="" data-test-id="thought-header" class="thought-header gds-body-m gds-italic">Linking Administrative Conduct to Findings</div><div _ngcontent-ng-c672901147="" data-test-id="thought-body" class="gds-body-m gds-italic">I am now transitioning to a forensic review of previous interview records to identify any prior administrative admissions that contradict the current narrowing of the case. I intend to build a comprehensive evidentiary map that explicitly links every excluded witness to specific regulatory breaches, ensuring that the investigator cannot ignore the interconnected nature of the events in the final report. My next steps involve finalizing a strategy to formally preserve the record of these omitted participants to ensure they are addressed in the eventual findings of fact.</div><!----></thought-item></div><!----><!----><!----><!----><!----><!----><div _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" class="item-container ng-tns-c542629960-29 ng-star-inserted" style=""><div _nghost-ng-c672901147=""><div _ngcontent-ng-c672901147="" data-test-id="thought-header" class="thought-header gds-body-m gds-italic">Administrative Responsibility and Impartiality</div><div _ngcontent-ng-c672901147="" data-test-id="thought-body" class="gds-body-m gds-italic">I am synthesizing the specific mandate within the governing student grievance procedure which requires the university to address any raised concerns of bias or conflict of interest regarding investigators. My research into federal case processing standards suggests that while an initial notice may be general, an investigation is legally required to be thorough and dispositive of all allegations. This means that if new compliance concerns emerge during the fact-finding process, the final report must address them regardless of whether they were included in the initial notice, establishing a procedural safety net against a prematurely narrowed scope.</div><!----></thought-item></div><div _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" class="item-container ng-tns-c542629960-29 ng-star-inserted" style=""><div _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" class="item-side ng-tns-c542629960-29"><!----><mat-divider _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" role="separator" class="mat-divider item-side-line ng-tns-c542629960-29 mat-divider-vertical" aria-orientation="vertical"></mat-divider></div><thought-item _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" class="ng-tns-c542629960-29" _nghost-ng-c672901147=""><div _ngcontent-ng-c672901147="" data-test-id="thought-header" class="thought-header gds-body-m gds-italic">The Adequacy of Notice</div><div _ngcontent-ng-c672901147="" data-test-id="thought-body" class="gds-body-m gds-italic">I am thinking through the contradiction between the investigator's claim that the current notice is merely 'general' and state-level guidance requiring that notice be sufficient for a respondent to understand the full nature of the claims. If the notice is too narrow to allow respondents to answer for specific failures to accommodate, the entire process risks being structurally flawed. I am evaluating how to resolve this by ensuring that the evidence already submitted serves as a 'constructive notice' that the university cannot ignore without violating its own standards for a prompt and equitable resolution.</div><!----></thought-item></div><div _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" class="item-container ng-tns-c542629960-29 ng-star-inserted" style=""><div _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" class="item-side ng-tns-c542629960-29"><!----><mat-divider _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" role="separator" class="mat-divider item-side-line ng-tns-c542629960-29 mat-divider-vertical" aria-orientation="vertical"></mat-divider></div><thought-item _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" class="ng-tns-c542629960-29" _nghost-ng-c672901147=""><div _ngcontent-ng-c672901147="" data-test-id="thought-header" class="thought-header gds-body-m gds-italic">Evidentiary Mapping and Policy Verification</div><div _ngcontent-ng-c672901147="" data-test-id="thought-body" class="gds-body-m gds-italic">I am transitioning to a detailed analysis of newer procedural guidance mentioned during the intake process, specifically focusing on whether parties have a right to review a summary of evidence before the final report is drafted. My next steps involve creating a rigorous evidentiary map that links every excluded staff member to specific administrative failures. By explicitly documenting these connections now, I intend to verify that the final investigation report adheres to the promise of reflecting the complete theory of the case, thereby preventing the omission of key discriminatory patterns during the final determination.</div><!----></thought-item></div><!----><!----><!----><!----><!----><!----><div _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" role="separator" class="mat-divider item-side-line ng-tns-c542629960-29 is-first mat-divider-vertical" aria-orientation="vertical"></mat-divider></div><thought-item _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" class="ng-tns-c542629960-29" _nghost-ng-c672901147=""><div _ngcontent-ng-c672901147="" data-test-id="thought-header" class="thought-header gds-body-m gds-italic">Expanded Investigative Scope</div><div _ngcontent-ng-c672901147="" data-test-id="thought-body" class="gds-body-m gds-italic">I’ve discovered that federal and state investigative standards allow for a case's scope to broaden beyond the initial formal notice if new evidence of misconduct is uncovered during the fact-finding stage. This means the investigator's refusal to amend the charges now does not legally preclude them from making findings of fact against the excluded individuals later. I am synthesizing how this flexibility provides a critical path for ensuring that the full extent of the administrative failures is captured in the final determination, regardless of the current narrow notice.</div><!----></thought-item></div><div _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" class="item-container ng-tns-c542629960-29 ng-star-inserted" style=""><div _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" class="item-side ng-tns-c542629960-29"><!----><mat-divider _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" role="separator" class="mat-divider item-side-line ng-tns-c542629960-29 mat-divider-vertical" aria-orientation="vertical"></mat-divider></div><thought-item _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" class="ng-tns-c542629960-29" _nghost-ng-c672901147=""><div _ngcontent-ng-c672901147="" data-test-id="thought-header" class="thought-header gds-body-m gds-italic">Impartiality and Conflict Protocols</div><div _ngcontent-ng-c672901147="" data-test-id="thought-body" class="gds-body-m gds-italic">I am resolving a significant contradiction between the investigator's discretion to limit the case's scope and the specific university mandate to address concerns of bias or conflict of interest. A specific historical resolution agreement requires the institution to resolve bias claims regarding those handling discrimination complaints in a timely manner. This suggests that if the current narrowing of the investigation is interpreted as a lack of impartiality, there is a formal mechanism to challenge that direction before the final report is issued, rather than waiting for an appeal.</div><!----></thought-item></div><div _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" class="item-container ng-tns-c542629960-29 ng-star-inserted" style=""><div _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" class="item-side ng-tns-c542629960-29"><!----><mat-divider _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" role="separator" class="mat-divider item-side-line ng-tns-c542629960-29 mat-divider-vertical" aria-orientation="vertical"></mat-divider></div><thought-item _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" class="ng-tns-c542629960-29" _nghost-ng-c672901147=""><div _ngcontent-ng-c672901147="" data-test-id="thought-header" class="thought-header gds-body-m gds-italic">Mapping Evidence to Outcomes</div><div _ngcontent-ng-c672901147="" data-test-id="thought-body" class="gds-body-m gds-italic">I am now shifting my focus to constructing a comprehensive evidentiary map that explicitly links the excluded staff members to specific policy violations. By formalizing how each witness proffer supports the overarching theory of a continuous pattern of discrimination, I intend to verify that the investigator cannot omit these elements from the final analysis. My next steps involve analyzing the specific interview transcripts to identify admissions that can be leveraged to ensure the final report reflects the full complexity of the failure to provide accommodations.</div><!----></thought-item></div><!----><!----><!----><!----><!----><!----></div><div _ngcontent-ng-c542629960="" class="skeleton-loader-container ng-tns-c542629960-29 ng-trigger
```
